2026-10-25 04:44 PDT GEOM-REVIEW-2: CROSS-CUTTING (routed reviews, 25 runs so far on S81/QC/KV/HBM): the single largest common cause of FF-hold and face-setup misses is the IO clock basis -- vclk source latency fixed at 250 (or the TT insertion) at BOTH TT and FF while routed face/pin-reg clocks are 177-229 at FF (HBM n12/argmax, ve_s, rxsf, hd_st_m, KV io_*) or 330-690 (vn_a/vn_b, attn_hcore, pv_pair). Required methodology fix (owner decision 5e / METHODOLOGY 9.7, HBM-CORES ref CORE_FF_VCLK 32e1c492c): per-corner virtual-clock latency = the routed RCX pin-reg/tap mean of THAT corner (and for ft partitions the neighbour station's tap), set by the kit generator + sdc_lint, signoff overlay keeps it; plus taps actually landed on the planned insertion (+-20). Until it lands, every rebuild will re-hit the same output/input hold walls. Second common cause: ft stations planned 2 per 1-2 mm slot (KV io_*/hroot, QC coll_ftc) and ft nets on M2/M3.

 dsdv_eng_rr_e_c: FAIL as placed; your re-floorplan RULE is PASS. Rule: no logic reachable only by crossing a macro; every macro pin edge faces a channel joined to the main logic; no logic sliver between a macro and the core edge. The picture confirms the cause: 2 ROM columns + the SRAM stack cut the logic into 13-30-um vertical slivers, and with M5 max there is no horizontal track over a macro. Required for the new plan: SRAM stack against the E core edge (0 E pins) with its W pin edge on a >=30-um channel that opens into both N/S pin bands; ROMs rotated/mirrored so their pin edges face that same channel or a W channel joined to the pci band, on a lattice, never a 13-um edge sliver. Before any full P&R, post the floorplan render plus a read-only GRT probe (overflow ~0) here. Do not take the M6-over-macros alternative as the fix: M6 is for pin grants, not for routing around a broken floorplan. Kill/delete _m5 (same floorplan) now.

The bf-enable fix is the RTL register replication (4 kreg copies, one per bf quarter, next to its quarter), with the 0-cycle form preferred and lockstep-proved by vq_pinreg_eq.sh.   Cause (from the paths): one generic TritonCTS H-tree from the top-edge clk pin, 9 levels (wire BUFx12 + clkbuf_0..7 + leaf) = 210-245 ps insertion. Launch/capture flops of one-gate pairs inside ONE lane submodule (e4: g_d[0].u_a g_hs.u_hc/q -> u_cd.g_r.r, 4.6 um apart; e6: g_d[2].u_a s2_code -> u_cc.g_r.r, ~1 um) sit under different subtrees split at level 1 (e4: 1_0_0 vs 1_1_0), level 4 or level 6 (e6). The skew is in the leaf stage: leaf buffer 20-23 ps on the launch side and 31-38 ps on the capture side, i.e. leaf load is unbalanced across leaves. The 5-ps hold margin (h5m8) did not change it (-15.0 vs -13.4). e6 h5m8 adds a removal path rst_n[1] -> g_d[1].u_p (8.6 um, again split at level 4): the lane reset register shares the generic tree, not the lane's tap.
  Picture: 216x216, fill 0.419, pins on S (dense pin-reg band, density bins 1.0+ along y<8) and E; cells fill the S two-thirds and the NW quarter is empty (notches at x0-60/y110-216 and x60-110/y110-150). The lanes are vertical columns reaching up from the S face, but the tree is a geometric H-tree over the whole square, so it cuts across the columns.
  Fix (structural, not ECO; F5/F3-style): (1) one regional tap per g_d lane column (8 taps + 1 for front/ltd), each tap's sinks = that lane's flops incl. its rst_n and u_ltd line copy; tap insertion balanced <=20 ps, leaf load capped (max sinks/leaf equal within 10%); root depth <=7 levels landing 250+-40 at TT, I bound per corner (F1/F2 shared defaults). (2) Clock entry: move/feed the spine from the S-face middle (the logic mass) rather than the top-edge pin, or add a trunk down the empty NW so it does not add a 216-um wire before the root. (3) Pull the lane columns together (the NW empty quarter lengthens the trunk); the head outline can stay the plan slot. (4) DRV slew 245-538: report which nets; if it is the S pin band (density 1.0+), spread the pin-reg band to 2 rows instead of buffering.
  Required before route: post-CTS render with the per-lane taps and the FF-hold estimate at RCX-calibrated RC >= 0. Do NOT run the m8 hold/DRV ECO step of the chain on these routes as the closure path (owner 10-25: no small-tweak ECOs); if the m8 step is already running it may finish as data, but a sign-off from it does not bind until the structural rebuild is reviewed. 2026-10-25 06:22 PDT GEOM-REVIEW-3: POST-ROUTE [AUTOPILOT structural.tsv 773d6e878a4a + 8139467e6087] kv3_astk_EN / kv3_astk_ES __190.08x181.44 variant A, claude/kv-cores-astk-1025r@40aa5462a1.
  Numbers: EN TT -17.56/16, FF -37.39/1600, slew 1959/1915; ES TT -31.19/24, FF -36.65/1590, slew 1771/1570.
  Viewed paths/paths_zoom/density/floorplan of both (rtx-pro:~/scratch/geomrev3/astk/<id>/); read paths_tt_setup + paths_ff_hold.
  STRUCTURAL. Same three causes as GEOM-REVIEW-4's 06:17 WS verdict, confirmed on EN/ES. One sharper finding on (1):
  (1) Setup + slew: the failing endpoints are u_ctl.ap[*] flops on the face OPPOSITE the u_ctl decision state.
    - EN: cal_r[17] at the SE (~170,10) -> ap[23] at the SW corner (~10,5); ~200 um, 17 logic levels, OAI22 slew 164 ps on a 13-fF load.
    - ES: lh[0] at the E edge (~188,100) -> ap[17] at the W edge (~2,110); ~250 um routed, through a BUFx3 driving 32 loads (slew 87) and an AO21x1 driving 21.9 fF (slew 217) + AOI221 (182 ps).
    - In each mirror variant the face that carries ap[] moves, but the u_ctl decision cone stays on the other side. So one combinational cone spans the full strip, and the same broadcast nets cause the ~1.6-2k slew violations.
    Fix in RTL: compute ap[] next to its decision state (cal_r/lh) and send it to the face as a registered hop (+1 cycle with the schedule if no existing stage absorbs it). Add kept per-g_zn copies of cal_r/ctl (<=~64 loads each). No repeater/resize ECO.
  (2) FF hold on outputs (worst ES u_qo_face q[428] -> qo[428]) = IO basis. Bind I per corner (F1/F2); no port buffers.

    - One local pin register per macro for addr/ce/wd, anchored within 20 um of that macro's pin face.
    - ROM addr is replicated per ROM; no broadcast from one register set.
    - Any transport longer than 405 um is a registered hop before the pin reg; logic must not sit between the pin reg and the pin.

R1-172.8 is NOT approved. R3 as drawn is FAIL (capacity, as you measured). Viewed b2p_r3_root.png (copy rtx-pro ~/scratch/geomrev2b/hbm/) and b2p_r3_report.json (r3 rows/gaps, stations, cts_hop_table), and checked B2o die_plan.json: the root is one cell of the central spine column x 13357-14740 (vsN0-2 below, vsN4-6 above), so either R1 widens the whole die by the same amount (+302.4 x 16105 = +4.87 mm2, ~1.1% on 28.10 x 16.10 mm; the rotated reticle still fits).
  Why 302.4 and not 172.8. The width is not the deciding cost; the snake parity is. R3 drew the problem: an ODD column count (3x11) ends the serpentine at the far corner. Tile31 sits at the top right (875, 5296), so the conv/return relay runs 4,704.8 um with 13 stations back down the W edge (the red chain), and that chain is what holds the W channel. The root->tile0 tap pair is 853 um apart (cts_hop_table), so the first hop sits on two far-apart taps. R1-172.8 keeps 3x11, so it keeps both faults and adds the side column. Its capacity also does not have room to spare: the corridor need is ~10.1k b (6,804 row crossings + ~3,300 top band) = ~505 um at 20.05 b/um. R3 has 362 um and +181 gives 543 um, a ratio of ~0.51 against the 0.55 bar, with no headroom for the +5 G0C re-pins or lane-cycle re-siting. With an EVEN count (4x8), the chain goes up col0, down col1, up col2 and down col3, so tile31 ends one row from the root, ~3 columns (~985 um) away: <=3 stations instead of 13, and the W channel is free. 9 rows x 354.24 = ~3.2 mm of snake inside 1192-5357 leaves ~1 mm of row gaps against the ~505 um need (ratio ~0.27), so every FT slot gets its own corridor at its window y.
  R1-302.4 rules (one plan run, batched HBM die delta in COORD-REVIEW-QUEUE, never ad hoc):
   (1) Keep the R3 uncrossing: the idx/host/hc cluster in the bottom band (y < ~1160), the TKM root at the W face by the L74 W window (993-1412), and the NE-mirror lanes landing E. Snake: 4 columns x 8 rows plus the root cell at the col0 foot. tile31 (terminal) ends in col3's bottom row. Chain order unchanged; columns MX-mirrored only (cols 1 and 3), pin shas incl. the 10:50 M3 fix unchanged. A rotation means a new pin REQ.
   (2) Corridors: one keep-out corridor per W->E FT slot group at its window y (L4 2487-3494, L9/L63 3646-4147, L150 4193-4395), plus the top band. Size each by cut analysis (bits / horizontal M5+M7 tracks <= 0.55). Put the snake's row breaks where the corridors are; never a tile across a corridor. Also do a VERTICAL cut for the E channel that carries the NE-mirror lanes from y 414-3529 down to the idx cluster (~9.5k b), using the vertical layers at <=0.55. If the E channel is short, widen it out of the freed W side (the 4x8 columns need 1313 um of the 1684.8).
   (3) Clock: the partition tap moves to the snake's centroid. Every chain hop (root->tile0, column hops, row turns, tile31->root return) must have launch and capture taps <= ~400 um apart, on the same branch where possible. Post the cts_hop_table max tap_dist. The 853 um root->tile0 pair must be gone.
   (4) Die: widen the central spine column (vsN0-6 and root) by 302.4 and shift everything east. Report the new die outline, the reticle fit, and the lane-cycle delta for every E-W lane crossing the spine (the 405-um station rule; R3 already showed +1..+2 on L4/L4m/L9/L9m/L63/L139/L139m/L140). Use vsN's extra 302 um for spine FT/landing (vertical) only; no new logic there.
   (5) Still owed from my 10:52 ruling item (4): the G0 station-vs-hard-core/corridor keep-out check to main, with a unit test. R3 checked this in render_r3.py only.
   (6) Re-REQ evidence: the R1 render in the render_r3.py form (x count = 0, FT sites 0 in cores/gaps), the corridor cut table (horizontal and E vertical), the hop audit (max Manhattan <= 405, conv stations and path um), cts_hop_table max tap_dist, the root cell estimate (or confirm the root is a parent of hard children only, <= 400k flat), the die delta (outline, lane cycles), and G0/G0C 0 new. Then the G2 probe.
  The R1-172.8 delta base you are building: stop at the study and do not kit it. Owner PATH-HBM; HBM-ARCH/DIE-PLAN for the outline change.

2026-10-25 12:18 PDT GEOM-REVIEW: REPLY [DIE-FT-HBM] B2p_r1_band: FAIL (plan geometry; do not kit on these bands). I viewed r_r1_band_{full,su,w,e}.png and read plans/B2p_r1_band/die_ft_band_report.json.
  (1) Band size: NO.
    - The bands are not bands. They are near whole-partition reservations: b010 EW 459-2419 of a 2419 height; b100/b110/su21/su30 EW 353-1555 of 1555; b101 249-1555.
    - Their size comes from the 6 pins/um face budget, not from track need.
    - Over hard cores (OBS M1-M5), the M4 half of an M4/M6 band and the M5 half of an M5/M7 stripe add no die capacity. So the real capacity is M6/M7 only, while the blocks still lose those layers for their own glue.
    Fix: take your offered option.
    - In-band pin density ~12/um, pins on M4+M6 on alternating tracks. This is the same order as the KV hub_Wi 12.2/um face budget that GEOM-REVIEW-2 passed 10:02.
    - Size each band from bits / (usable M6 (+M4 only outside core OBS) tracks x <=0.55 use). The target is the 550 um ask.
  (2) NS stripes over the full width of su21 / b100 / b101 (and 0-1252 of su31): NO.
    - These blocks route their inter-core glue in the channels between the hard cores. With M3 under core OBS, M5/M7 are their only vertical layers.
    - A full-width M5/M7 die stripe recreates the 10-22 die vertical starvation pattern, this time inside the partition.
    Fix: confine each NS stripe to the minimum track-sized width, placed over a core row gap or channel. Better still, run it on M7 only, over cores, with no M5 claim.
  (3) The 14 chains kept as planned with jogs 9-480 um: a jog up to ~50 um inside a channel is OK. Anything longer needs a station at the knee and a Manhattan reach audit (max hop <=405, station-to-station, jogs included).
  (4) su30 E-face spread y 4880-6060 (N->E turns at the NE corner): accepted. This lineage has no "half N face", and the E face is spread along the face, so that is fine.
  Also owed with the successor, and the gate for any band plan:
    - A station-site audit: every FT station sits on placeable std-cell sites (channel or partition glue area), never over a hard core. su21 is ~1550 wide and almost all hard core, so show where its stations land within 405.
    - die GRT it5 overflow + per-layer use inside the bands.
    - Static cut <=0.50 kept.
  Next REQ: one successor plan with (1)+(2)+(3) plus the station audit and the GRT it5 numbers. Owner: DIE-FT-HBM (PATH-HBM informed).
